Um, yeah, I guess I have noticed that a little bit. I hope it's okay to admit this, but... well, I used to get really excited about things. Um... well, it's a little hard to talk about, honestly. I mean, I don't want to sound ungrateful, because my manager actually gave me this Employee of the Month award recently, and they even mentioned I might have a chance to be a supervisor. Which is... it's really nice of them.

I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds silly. I really should just be happy about it, right? I hope it's okay that I feel this way... I'm trying to work on it, I promise.

Um... yeah, I guess I have been a little tired lately. I mean, I don't want to complain, because my job isn't even that hard, really. I just work in retail. But... I don't know, by the middle of a shift, my body just starts to feel so heavy.

I try really hard to push through it because I don't want my manager to think I'm slacking off, especially after they gave me that award. I always make sure I'm still smiling for the customers. 

But I definitely feel... drained, I guess? Even on my days off when I'm just sitting around. I hope I don't just sound like I'm out of shape or not trying hard enough. Is it okay that I feel that way?

Um... yeah, I guess there's been a little bit of a change. I really hope it's not a big deal, and I know I should be taking better care of myself, but... honestly, this is kind of what made me finally ask for help.

I try really hard to push through it because I don't want my manager to think I'm slacking off, especially after they gave me that award. I always make sure I'm still smiling for the customers. \n\nBut I definitely feel... drained, I guess? Even on my days off when I'm just sitting around. I hope I don't just sound like I'm out of shape or not trying hard enough. Is it okay that I feel that way?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Have these feelings lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's been a little while now, I think. I really hope I'm not making a bigger deal out of this than it is. But... yeah, I guess it's been going on for most of the day, pretty much every day, for about two months now. \n\nIt kind of started around the time I got that award for the window display... and when the district manager started paying more attention to me. It's just... that's kind of when this heavy, tired feeling started sticking around all the time. \n\nI've been trying my best to snap out of it so I don't let anyone down. I hope it's not frustrating that it's been going on for that long... I really am trying to get better. I really hope it's not a big deal, and I know I should be taking better care of myself, but... honestly, this is kind of what made me finally ask for help. \n\nI just... I barely want to eat anymore. I don't want to sound ungrateful for having food or anything, but like, I'll be on my lunch break at the store, and I'll have something I usually really like. But I just sit there staring at it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, since I haven't been eating much like I mentioned... yeah, I guess I have lost a little bit of weight. I haven't been trying to or anything, I swear! I know I'm supposed to be keeping my strength up for work. 

I don't have a scale at home, so I don't know exactly how much, but... my work pants are definitely a lot looser on me than they were a couple of months ago. I actually had to punch a new hole in my belt just so they wouldn't look baggy. I really hope my manager doesn't think I look unprofessional or anything. 

I know I should be doing a better job of forcing myself to eat so I don't lose too much weight... I'm sorry, I really am trying. Is it okay that I lost weight just because I haven't had much of an appetite?

I try to push through it so my manager doesn't think I'm slacking off, but it's just really hard right now. \n\nIs that normal? I mean, I hope I'm not making a big deal out of nothing... is that the kind of tired you meant?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have been moving more slowly than usual, or that your body feels physically slowed down, like you're moving through molasses?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think so? I mean, I try really hard to stay on top of things, especially at work. I don't want anyone thinking I'm holding up the line or, you know, not pulling my weight. \n\nI guess I haven't really noticed feeling like I'm moving through molasses or anything like that. My thoughts feel pretty normal to me, I think. But maybe I'm just not paying close enough attention? I hope that's an okay answer. If you need me to think about it more, I definitely can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel unusually restless or that you can’t sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I've been feeling restless or anything. I wouldn't want to make anyone else nervous or uncomfortable by fidgeting all the time, you know? \n\nI can sit still just fine, I think. Which is... it's really nice of them. \n\nBut... I just keep thinking I don't really deserve it. Like, I feel this weird guilt, almost like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I'm doing a better job than I actually am. My coworkers keep telling me I earned it and that I shouldn't think that way, and I try to smile and say thank you so I don't seem rude or unappreciative. But inside... I just feel like I'm a fake. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds silly. I really should just be happy about it, right? I hope it's okay that I feel this way... I'm trying to work on it, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in most activities, even things you used to really enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean... I guess I've been a little less active lately? I hope that's not a bad thing. I used to really love doing stuff, you know? Like going to gallery nights, or playing basketball with the guys, or even just grabbing food-truck tacos. I also used to help out with some art stuff for the kids in the neighborhood, which was always really fun.\n\nBut recently... I don't know. I try to go and be part of it so I don't let anyone down, but when I'm there, I just kind of stand around feeling... flat, I guess? Like the excitement just isn't there anymore. Oh... um, no. No, definitely not. I really hope I didn't say anything to make you think I was... you know, wanting to hurt myself or anything like that. I promise I haven't had any thoughts about dying.

I really hope it doesn't make me sound ungrateful, because I know I should just be happy about the award and the district manager noticing me. It's silly, I know, because people keep telling me I did a good job, but... I just feel this awful guilt, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I'm better at my job than I actually am. \n\nI really try not to let it show, because I don't want to seem like I'm complaining when good things happen. I know I should just accept it and do my best. Is it normal to feel like a fraud when people give you a compliment? I'm sorry if that's not very helpful, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is it okay that this is the first time it's happened?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death, dying, or not wanting to live anymore?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, no. No, definitely not.
